**Independent review.** A sub-agent with fresh context audited M0 against the specs and found
**2 blockers, 7 majors and 18 minors**. Everything it found is fixed; every fix carries the test
that would have caught it. The two blockers were both guards reporting success while enforcing
nothing — the test-time network guard let real HTTP and all of DNS out of a unit test, and the core
purity ban on `Date` and `process` had been silently replaced by ESLint flat config's
options-replacement semantics. Full detail in *Plan changes*.

That makes **nine** guards and gates in this repository caught proving nothing during M0. Two of
them were found while closing the gate itself: the plan checker's 54 unit tests — the ones that
prove it reports an error rather than passing over a broken plan — were referenced by nothing and
had never run in CI; and the release workflow's version-PR dry run had been green all milestone
over an **empty** `.changeset/`, so the first changeset ever written broke `changeset version` on
`main` within a minute of this PR merging. The pattern in every case: the guard was proven to work *at its own entry point*, or
*in isolation*, or *against its own configuration*, or not proven to **run** at all — never
against the real command in the real tree. The rule that now governs
all of them, and the one thing from M0 worth carrying into every later milestone:

> Plant the violation the way a contributor would write it, in the real tree, and make the real
> command be the thing that fails.

**Closed out after the merge, 2026-11-03.** Merged as `7081e54` (a **merge** commit, not a squash,
so the per-task hashes above stay resolvable on `main`), then four more defects, and `main` green on
`e2ebfb9` across all eleven workflows: `ci`, `codeql`, `docs`, `docs-deploy`, `edge`, `osv`,
`release`, `scorecard`, `supply-policy`, `zizmor` (`docs-preview` skipped, by design — it is a
`workflow_run` trigger).

The fourth was a gate gap rather than a bug: **`docs.yml` was never a required status check**, so
when the offline link check failed on a broken link in this very file, the pull request stayed
mergeable. `M0-T16`'s "lychee offline link check passes" and all of `M0-T18`'s docs CI were
therefore advisory. Its job is now `docs-ok`, its path filter is gone, and it is the **eighth**
required check.

Three defects in the release path, all found by
running `changeset version` for real for the first time — the milestone PR carried the first
changeset in this repository's history, and it failed on `main` within a minute of merging. The
formatter path in `.changeset/config.json` was resolvable from the repository root but not from
`.changeset/`, where Changesets resolves it; the version-PR dry run had been green all milestone
because it was versioning an empty set; and the commit link landed in the middle of a sentence in
the first `CHANGELOG.md` this project ever generated. Fixed, with the four tests that resolve the
config option the way Changesets does. Numbers above are re-measured on the final tree.

**Cut-lines used.** None. `M0-T13` (Renovate), `M0-T14` (Changesets), `M0-T15` (names manifest) and
`M0-T22` (naming kickoff) were all allowed to slip to M3 and all landed in M0.

**Known gaps, stated rather than glossed.**

1. **The merge queue is off** and needs **H-6**. Mitigated by "require branches to be up to date".
2. **`pnpm verify`'s last step carries no evidence yet.** `evals:smoke` is a pending stub until
   `M3-T48`, so a green `verify` says nothing about evals. It exits 0 with a notice, as `M0-T1`'s
   AC permits; `M3-T48` must make the suite's absence a failure. Five other `CLAUDE.md` §4 commands
   are pending stubs on the same pattern (see Plan changes).
3. **No fresh-user dry run.** `M0-T16`'s community profile and `M0-T18`'s docs are checked by
   machine (GitHub community profile 100%, 170 internal links, 147 prose files) but no human
   stranger has read them. Offered as a follow-up; the strongest automated substitute is in place.
4. **Trademark clearance is a substitute, not an opinion.** ADR-024 records that every formal
   register was unreachable without an account, a key or a CAPTCHA, and that Trademarkia's zero
   result is strong evidence but not clearance. One residual (VIVO, class 9) is flagged for an
   attorney.
5. **Docs deploy is a no-op** until **H-9** (Cloudflare). Both deploy workflows exit cleanly with a
   summary saying so, rather than failing.
6. **The plan still uses the codename** in ~1,700 places. Deliberate, logged as a reading
   convention rather than a 2.5 MB rewrite; the two places where it is a *design* problem rather
   than a spelling one (`villageApi`, `kind='village'`) are renamed in the specs now, before either
   exists.
7. **One unexplained flake, once.** A Vitest worker crashed mid-run on a local machine (1034 of
   1044 tests reported); 0 of 5 retries reproduced it and it has not recurred in ~30 full runs
   since. Attributed to local resource pressure. **No retries were added** — a retry in a required
   check is how a real flake becomes invisible.

**What M0 bought.** A monorepo where the boundaries are machine-checked, a test harness that cannot
reach the network, a logger that content cannot get into, CI that runs in 75 seconds, a name that
is actually free, a rename that is one command, and 1117 tests of which a large fraction exist to
make a guard fail. Nothing in it is a prototype.

**Next: M1 — Storage & domain core.** No stop (`CLAUDE.md` §1.4.5). Entry criteria: M0 gate green
✓. SP-1/SP-2/SP-3 run first; the Raspberry Pi leg of SP-1 and SP-2 is an owner ask with an
automated substitute ready if hardware is unavailable.

### M1 — Storage & domain core

Status: **in progress** (started 2026-11-03) · Tasks: 27/35 · Gate: —

- [x] M1-T1 `@comvivo/storage` scaffold & pins — seven versions **re-verified against the registry** before pinning (`CLAUDE.md` §6): six are the current `latest`, and `temporal-polyfill` stays at 1.0.6 because 1.0.7 was published hours ago and `minimumReleaseAge` refuses it — the policy working as designed rather than as a theory. Exact pins in the catalog with `packages/storage/src/pins.test.ts` asserting each one has **no range operator at all**, because a caret here is what `pnpm up` produces and a SQLite build, a loadable extension and a crypto library are where a silent patch bump changes the bytes in a host's database. `drizzle.config.ts` per `02` §3.1, typechecked and type-aware-linted (proven by planting a floating promise in it). Two new guards for the `…Unfiltered` reads — an import rule and a call-shape rule, each proven to bite in the real tree through the real command. **Two defects found:** every package's `test` script bypassed the harness, and the plan's `…Unfiltered` rule could not be written as specified. 20 tests here, **1147 total**, 97.24% statements
- [x] M1-T2 Storage/encryption/index spike (SP-1, SP-3) — **ship D8**: every criterion in `02` §3.6.3's decision matrix passes, so the §3.6.5 plaintext fallback is unused and `VACUUM INTO` snapshots are encrypted (SP-3), which also retires `01` §14 A8's online-backup-API alternative. **U2 is the one that mattered**: 10,000 canaries across a table, both FTS tokenizers and vec0 appear in no file in three encodings — including in the `-wal` *while the connection is still open*, which the first version of that test missed because closing checkpoints the WAL. `04:M1-T61` (a)–(d) all pass, each asserted in the direction that matters (an empty eligible set returns nothing; every constraint drops exactly the row it should). Twenty `SIGKILL`s mid-write: `integrity_check` ok, **no committed row lost**, verified against the child's own record of commits rather than a count. A worker read connection reads successive snapshots while the main thread writes. **Five defects and one changed default** — the 100× page-cache cliff, the Drizzle dependency alias, `trusted_schema`, the vec0 rowid binding, and hidden type declarations — all in Plan changes. Kept as a **68-test** regression suite in `pnpm verify`, **green on darwin-arm64 and linux-x64 × Node 26.13.0 and 24.22.2**, because every fact here is a fact about somebody else's native module. Report: [`storage-spike.md`](../architecture/storage-spike.md), numbers: [`storage-benchmarks.md`](../architecture/storage-benchmarks.md), ADR-005 addendum landed. **U7 and SP-2's gate need a Pi (H-10)**; `scripts/bench/pi.sh` is the one-command session, and the darwin-arm64 numbers are labelled a ceiling rather than evidence. **1220 tests total**, 97.11% statements
- [x] M1-T3 IDs, clock & canonical JSON — the `02` §3.1 registry (61 prefixes, each asserted legal under TypeID's own rule, all distinct, cross-checked against `typeid-js` for every one), `parseId` returning a `Result` and **never echoing its input** (a malformed id is usually a pasted credential), `idSchema`, `SystemClock` truncating to the millisecond storage can hold, `SystemTimers` that cannot leak a handle, `TypeIdGenerator`, `SystemRng`, and RFC 8785 canonical JSON. **11 properties** over UUIDv7 — the one that matters is that ids sort lexicographically in creation order through a 200-id burst and across a backwards clock step, because the turn journal and the outbox read id order *as* sequence. **Three defects found:** the port let an id be branded as the wrong entity, `brandedId` accepted suffixes `typeid-js` refuses, and the `M0-T7` suites were minting ids from **entity names** (`'turn'`, `'out'`) that no parser would accept. 168 tests here, **1398 total**, 97.62% statements
- [x] M1-T4 Crypto primitives + SP-2 — `shared/crypto` is now the **one** directory that may import `node:crypto` or libsodium (`08` PSC-8, enforced by dependency-cruiser and proven by a planted violation): CSPRNG, HKDF-SHA256 over `02` §3.6.1's key tree, AES-256-GCM wrapping with **mandatory** AAD, SHA-256/HMAC and keyed blob addresses, constant-time compare, scrypt for the one passphrase slot, secretstream, zeroing, and the frozen domain separators. `sec.crypto.kat` pins **RFC 5869 test cases 1–3** and the FIPS 180-4 SHA-256 examples — standards output, not our own behaviour recorded. `stream.contract.test.ts` runs the contract against **both** secretstream implementations (46 tests) and cross-decrypts in both directions, so `02` D13's "wire-identical" is measured. Tamper, **reorder**, **replay** and truncation all detected, with truncation-at-a-boundary and truncation-mid-chunk raising different errors on purpose. Buffer zeroing verified, including after a throw. **100% statements, branches, functions and lines** across the directory, with two unreachable defensive branches removed rather than suppressed. **Three defects found**, all in Plan changes — the specs' separators carried the dead codename, my keyring AAD was weaker than `08` §3.7.4, and `sodium-native` is not an API drop-in. **SP-2's 40 MB/s gate still needs a Pi (H-10)**; `libsodium-wrappers` measures 372 MB/s on this machine, which is nine times the threshold but not the threshold's hardware. 189 tests here, **1554 total**, 98.0% statements
- [x] M1-T5 KeyService & keyring — slots (master key, recovery key, optional passphrase) wrapping the root data key under `08` §3.7.4's **four-binding** AAD, the §3.7.5 unlock order (`COMVIVO_MASTER_KEY_FILE` → `COMVIVO_MASTER_KEY` → keychain → keyfile → passphrase) with the env variable **deleted after reading** (S9), HKDF salt = `instanceId`, the static `K_blobid` wrapped under the current generation, and the whole `08` §3.7.8 loss table. Writes are atomic — temp → fsync → preserve `.prev` → rename → fsync the directory — and **every one of the five steps is fault-injected**, each asserted to leave a host with a keyring they can open. Group- or world-readable key material refuses start; anything else auto-tightens to 0600/0700, measured against real `stat` modes rather than a fake's. The last recovery slot **cannot** be removed, per generation, because a retired generation whose recovery slot is gone is a set of backups nobody can restore. **Five defects found**, all in Plan changes — the one that matters is that `sealed` was validated for presence and not shape, so a damaged keyring was reported as a wrong key and the host sent to the recovery path instead of to `keyring.json.prev`. **212 tests here** (166 unit, 38 integration against a real filesystem), **1768 total**, 98.08% statements / 94.76% branches; the crypto directory is 98.36% statements with its only gaps three guards documented as unreachable-by-design — `b7c166b`
- [x] M1-T6 Recovery-key codec & key-operations library — the `VRK1-` codec (`08` §3.7.6): 52 Crockford characters carrying 256 bits with four zero padding bits, a 20-bit checksum over `"VRK1" ‖ key`, printed as 14 groups of four. **Every single-character substitution is refused — all 34,720 of them**, every position times every other character over 20 keys, asserted exhaustively rather than sampled, plus every adjacent transposition. Transcription is forgiving exactly where `08` §3.7.6 says: case, hyphens, spaces, newlines, and `I`/`L` → `1`, `O` → `0` — while `U` stays a typo. Nothing ever echoes the typed key, and a **typo is reported differently from a wrong key**, because the first means "look at your paper" and the second means "find a newer printout". The library implements all ten operations, each crash-safe: `status` (safe to paste into a support thread — asserted to contain no wrap and no salt), `verifyRecovery`, `rotateMaster`, `rotateData`, `rotateRecovery`, `rotateSessions`, `recover`, `addPassphrase`, `removeKeySlot`, `purgeRetired`. Every key-replacing operation is **two-phase with the old key live until the new one is durably stored**, and each is driven with a fault injected at all five write steps — 15 injected crashes, each asserted to leave a keyring something the host holds still opens, and each asserted to have actually fired. **Four defects and one spec gap found**, all in Plan changes; the gap is that `rotate-data` cannot build a valid generation without the recovery key, which `08` §3.7.7's step list does not mention. 115 tests here (34 codec, 59 operations, 22 ceremony copy), **1883 total**, 98.11% statements / 93.65% branches; `recovery-key.ts` is 100% on every metric — `19e1859`
- [x] M1-T7 Data-dir primitives — `12` §3.7's layout and modes (`layout.ts`, with `comvivo.db` and `comvivo.lock` derived from `BRAND` so a rename stays one command), the `FORMAT` marker (`format.ts` + the brand-guard-exempt `marker.ts`), the instance lock (`lock.ts`), the permission audit (`perms.ts`) and the filesystem check (`fstype.ts`). **`12` §3.8.1 step 2's three outcomes are the centre of it**: no marker and no data is a fresh install, a marker is read and version-checked, and **data with no marker exits 65** — because the only safe reading of "there is a database here and no marker I recognise" is "this is not mine". The lock implements the whole of `12` §3.7: `O_EXCL`, a 10 s heartbeat, takeover at 60 s of silence *or* a dead pid on the same host and boot, per-role lock files, and `STORAGE_LOCKED` naming the holder's pid, host and version. Driven by a **fake clock**, as the acceptance criterion asks — a real-clock takeover test would take a minute and be the flakiest test in the suite — and then by the real process in `lock.int.test.ts`. The audit implements all four `12` §3.7 outcomes and protects the asymmetry between them: readable key material is waivable with `--insecure-permissions` (`08` §3.7.3's ssh-style judgement), **writable key material never is**, because "another user may replace my master key" is not a privacy judgement. **Five findings and one of my own bad tests**, all in Plan changes — including a test whose premise I had not measured, which passed whether or not the code was right. 125 tests here (114 unit, 11 integration), **2008 total**, 97.61% statements / 93.01% branches — `69da4ff`
- [x] M1-T8 Database open/init — `openDatabase` with `02` §3.6.2's ten pragmas (**every one read back**, because an older SQLite ignores an unrecognised pragma instead of refusing it), `initDatabase`, worker connections and the `02` §4.3 error mapping. Wrong key → **`STORAGE_KEY_INVALID`**, and the hint tells the host *not* to restore over a database that is probably fine. The 192 MiB page cache is asserted as the correctness requirement the spike measured it to be — 508 ms against 5 ms on a 50k-vector KNN, 8× the `02` §3.16.4 budget — and `mmap_size` stays 0 because memory-mapped I/O bypasses the codec. Only the `main` role may create a file, so a `backup` command can never leave an empty database the next boot reads as a fresh install. **The worker reads while the main thread writes**, in a real `worker_threads` worker: the key arrives as a **transferred** `ArrayBuffer` — never `workerData`, never an env var — the sender's copy is detached by the transfer, and the worker's copy is zeroed on both the success and the failure path. **Six findings, two of them spec bugs**, all in Plan changes; the one that matters is that `auto_vacuum = INCREMENTAL` silently does nothing once WAL is set, so the spec's ordering would have shipped a product whose databases could never reclaim space. 82 tests here (41 unit, 41 integration), **2090 total**, 97.36% statements / 92.78% branches — `088e63b`
- [x] M1-T9 `SqliteStore`, `NotPromise` & the no-await-in-tx lint — `02` §3.7.1's `Store` port in `core` (types only, so `core` stays pure) and `02` §3.7.2's `SqliteStore` in storage. **`01` §1's rule is now four independent guards**, because better-sqlite3 commits when the callback *returns*: an `async` callback commits an empty transaction, does its work outside it with no isolation and no rollback, and **throws nothing**. `NotPromise<T>` makes it a compile error; a lint rule refuses it syntactically; `no-floating-promises` catches a promise created inside and not awaited; and a runtime thenable check throws `STORAGE_TX_ASYNC_CALLBACK` for the untyped caller. Any one can be defeated — a promise reaching SQLite cannot. `02` §9.3's two named tests are green: **`store.tx-async-guard`** (throws, commits nothing, runs no hooks — all three asserted) and **`store.nested-savepoint`** (an inner failure rolls back only the inner work when caught). `afterCommit` hooks run after the commit in order, are discarded by a savepoint rollback (F12), log and swallow their own failures, and a hook may open its own transaction without re-running the batch. A write inside a read is refused rather than silently promoted. Slow transactions log `storage.slow_tx` with the op and a duration, outermost only. **`db.transaction()` is now banned outside `tx.ts` and the spike**, so "`SqliteStore` is the only transaction entry" is enforced rather than conventional — both bans proven by planted fixtures, and the four-class flat-config composition they forced is asserted on **effective options** per path. **Six findings**, all in Plan changes; the one that matters is that a caller's own error was being swallowed into "the database failed". 52 tests here (30 store, 22 added to the lint-ban suite), **2142 total**, 97.39% statements / 92.63% branches — `6f782db`

The usage rollup property is asserted against a SQL oracle over `usage_records`, including a rolled-back transaction and a retried id. **Twenty-two mutations run against the suite; six were not caught on the first pass and each hole was closed with a test that fails without it** — including an I1 hole where deleting a trace's label filter left everything green, because the only test passed an *empty* eligible set, which an early return refuses before any SQL. **Six findings**, all in Plan changes; the two that reach beyond this task are that a hash chain verifying link by link cannot see the deletion it exists to detect, and that the empty-eligible-set test shape hides the filter under every labeled read written so far (both now deferred follow-ups with the work named). 3770 tests total, 96.86% statements / 90.55% branches / 98.74% functions, 398 modules and 1615 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `65a685a`
- [x] M1-T22 Outbox repository & state machine — `02` §3.9's state machine in `storage/src/outbox/{enqueue,repository}.ts` behind `core`'s `OutboxReader`/`OutboxRepository`, with the equal-jitter backoff in `core/src/runtime/backoff.ts` because `02` states one formula and §3.9 and §3.10.3 apply it with different constants. Every arrow in §3.9's diagram is a method and every method is tested: `enqueue`, `claim`, `markSent`, `markFailed`, `cancel`, `recoverSending`, `expire` (`06` §4.3's deadlines, and **never** `must_deliver`) and `retryDead`. **Both named tests green.** `outbox.fifo.property` asserts `02` §9.2 verbatim — per conversation, receipt order equals `seq` order — over generated scripts of timeouts, rate limits, lost leases and permanent failures, with non-vacuity asserted over the whole run rather than per case (a single script can legitimately deliver nothing, and asserting otherwise failed on exactly that counterexample). `outbox.crash.test` `SIGKILL`s a child at all **five** of §9.3's injection points, **10 iterations each — the 50 the spec names**, measured at 54.5 s — and asserts one `sent` row and one assistant message per idempotency key after the resume, with the fake platform's own journal as the record of what it received. **Twenty-five mutations run; three were not caught and each hole was closed** — the fence that `02` §3.9 specifies was shadowed by an equivalent pre-check and could be deleted wholesale, and the FIFO subquery could stop counting an in-flight head. **Five findings**, all in Plan changes; the two that change the spec are that `recoverSending` needs a boot mode (the outbox has no `lease_owner`, so "lease expired" and "boot" cannot share a predicate — found by the crash test resuming to zero sends) and that "no logical duplicate" has to be qualified, because one of §9.3's own kill points makes a physical duplicate unavoidable. 3808 tests total, 96.85% statements / 90.48% branches / 98.69% functions, 406 modules and 1677 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `b4bed98`
- [x] M1-T23 Durable job queue — `02` §3.10's two ports (`JobQueueTx` in the transaction, `JobQueue` for the worker loop) in `core/src/ports/storage/jobs.ts`, and `storage/src/queue/{kinds,queue,worker,error-info}.ts`. The kinds registry is `02` §3.10.1's catalog as **data** — all 54 kinds, transcribed row by row — joined with a payload schema each owner registers, because `02` says owners define those and a placeholder would be forty stubs. **All four acceptance criteria green.** `enqueueDebounced` moves `runAt` forward *and cannot pass* `debounce_deadline_at`, which is the half that stops a never-quiet chat from never being extracted. `singleFlightByKey` is tested against the window a leased job opens by freeing its debounce key — the only window in which two jobs can share one key. The model-based `fc.commands` suite runs enqueue/claim/complete/fail/recover/retryDead against a dictionary model and asserts no double-run, one row per idempotency key, and every backoff inside `[d/2, d]`. `queue.fairness` starts group B's job in the first round against group A's 1 000. **Thirty-six mutations run; seven were not caught and each hole was closed** — the three clauses of `02` §3.10.3's fence were all individually deletable, and the fairness ordering was invisible to a test with as many slots as partitions. **Five findings**, all in Plan changes; two reach well beyond this task: **eleven repositories added in `M1-T19`…`M1-T22` were never on `ReadScope` or `UnitOfWork`**, so nothing could reach them through a transaction, and **the test harness accepted an `async` callback and closed the database under it** — the same trap `02` §3.7.2 guards `store.write` against, now a type error in fourteen wrappers. 3859 tests total, 96.73% statements / 90.39% branches / 98.61% functions, 416 modules and 1752 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `5d3dba8`
- [x] M1-T24 Scheduler & Temporal schedule evaluator — `nextFireAfter` in `shared/src/time/schedule.ts` (pure, where `02` §3.10.5 puts it, taking a structural pattern so `shared` stays the inner layer) and `storage/src/queue/scheduler.ts`. The evaluator is a **field-wise** search rather than a minute-by-minute walk, because `0 9 30 2 *` has to be *proved* impossible rather than timed out; `daily` and `weekly` compile to the same matcher as `cron`, so a DST edge cannot behave differently for one than for the expression that means the same thing. **All seven of `02` §9.1's named cases green** — New York's spring gap and autumn overlap, a London weekly Sunday across a transition, Lord Howe's 30-minute shift, Kathmandu's 45-minute offset, Samoa's **missing calendar day**, and February 30th refused as `SCHEDULE_NO_NEXT` — plus `02` §3.10.5's four guarantees as properties over zone and instant generators weighted towards transition weekends. The scheduler resolves `TzRef` at fire time (never stored resolved, so a member who moves gets their brief at the new local time) and `recomputeFor` runs in the **same transaction** as the timezone change. **Twenty-three mutations run; four were not caught and each hole was closed.** **Three findings**, all in Plan changes, and the first is the session's most useful mistake: I removed `02` §3.10.5's `max(fire, now − 1 ms)` because deleting it changed no test, and it turns out to be the one thing stopping a punctual schedule from sticking one occurrence behind forever — with the idempotency key hiding the symptom. 3897 tests total, 96.73% statements / 90.37% branches / 98.64% functions, 421 modules and 1783 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `ec08128`
- [x] M1-T25 Blob cipher & blob store — `storage/src/crypto/blob-cipher.ts` (the `VBL1` file format) and `storage/src/blobs/*` (paths, store), behind `core/src/ports/storage/blobs.ts` so nothing above storage knows a blob is a file. The write path is `02` §3.11's in order — quota, free disk, fresh DEK, temp file, **stream** while hashing, address at EOF, per-address mutex, dedupe, `rename` + directory `fsync`, row — and never holds the plaintext: a 50 MiB attachment costs two 64 KiB buffers. **Content addressing is `HMAC(K_blobid, groupId ‖ 0 ‖ sha256)`**, so the same bytes dedupe inside a group and two instances holding the same file produce unrelated addresses; the plaintext hash appears nowhere under `data/blobs`, which is what makes the confirmation attack fail. Deletion is **crypto-shred**: the wrapped DEK is zeroed in the row first, so a half-finished delete leaves unreadable bytes rather than readable ones, and `rewrap` re-wraps every DEK under a new KEK generation without touching a body. **Twenty-nine mutations run; four were not caught and each hole was closed** (dedupe against a row already `deleting`, the GC grace period, the orphan sweep's existence guard, and `blobPath`'s address validation — the last one would have let a crafted address escape the blob root). **Four findings**, all in Plan changes: `08` §3.7.10's first-chunk AD is **uncomputable** by a content-addressed streaming writer, and the two specs now agree on the 8-byte prefix; `02` §3.11's "push(remaining **or empty**)" made this repo's two write paths produce different bytes for the same blob at exact 64 KiB multiples, fixed with one chunk of lookahead and a byte-identity test; the reserved prefix bytes were unchecked; and the AC's "raw SHA-256 never stored" was sharpened to cover the file contents, not only the filename. 3942 tests total, 96.66% statements / 90.36% branches / 98.07% functions, 428 modules and 1825 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `dcf8961`. **CI then found a thirtieth mutation that I had shipped**: the read framer accumulated pending bytes with `concat(pending, piece)`, which is quadratic in the number of reads — a 1 MiB blob arriving a byte at a time costs 34 GiB of `memcpy`. It surfaced as a 6-second unit test on the Node 24 leg, two seconds inside a 5-second timeout. Fixed to one fixed-capacity buffer with compaction, with a budget test that the quadratic version was re-run against to prove it fails (343 ms against 3 592 ms) — `1756335`
- [x] M1-T26 Retention & deletion primitives — `02` §3.13 whole: the daily sweep, the ten deletion primitives behind `02` §3.7.1's `uow.deletions`, the resumable group purge and the resumable person erasure. **All three acceptance criteria green** — `retention.cascade`, `group.purge`, `erasure.person` — across 71 tests in seven suites. Two design choices carry the task. The purge's table list is **introspected** from `sqlite_schema`, children before parents, rather than written down: a list needs editing by every milestone that adds a table and the failure would be *invisible*, because every group-scoped table cascades from `groups` and the final delete covers whatever the steps missed. So `group.purge` asserts the **per-step counts**, which the cascade cannot fake, and that is the only assertion that catches a table dropped from the plan. And `erasure.person`'s canary scan is **byte-level** over every text and blob column, the FTS doclists, the vec rows, the blob files and the raw database with its WAL, in four spellings — which is how it found that `secure-delete` leaves a deleted row's *terms* in the FTS segment dictionary, so a person's distinctive words survived their own erasure as recoverable bytes. An FTS `optimize` now ends all three paths and `02` §3.13.2 says so. **Closes `M1-T21`'s deferred audit-chain re-anchor**, as an `audit.reanchor` event inside the chain rather than a side table — and the mutation run then found two things the deferral had not: a later purge must take an earlier re-anchor with it, or it is stranded above a gap it does not vouch for; and the "keep one row" guard was both unnecessary and wrong. **Fifty-six mutations run; eighteen initially survived.** Fourteen were real holes, each closed with a named test; two drove the code changes above; one probe was vacuous; and three of my own probes were wrong rather than the tests being weak. 4014 tests total, 96.5% statements / 90.0% branches / 97.94% functions, 447 modules and 1965 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, green on Node **v26.13.0** (measured in the gate's own shell) — `5fbca6a`. **CI failed on the first push**: `retention/chunk.ts` missed the *per-file* coverage threshold (83.33% lines against 85%) because it carried an exported `countByColumn` nothing called. The failure was in my local `pnpm verify` output and I missed it by grepping for the lines I expected instead of checking the exit code — recorded in Plan changes, because the habit is the defect. Fixed by deleting the dead export
- [x] M1-T27 Backup library & `VBK1` envelope — `08` §3.7.11's container and `02` §3.14's pipeline: `crypto/backup-envelope.ts`, `crypto/stream-format.ts` and `backup/{manifest,create,restore}.ts`. **Every clause of the acceptance criterion green** across 58 tests: seal and open with the master key, with the printed recovery key and through the optional passphrase slot; **restore with the recovery key alone** (`01` §14 A10), arranged so the unlock holds that one key and nothing else, into a directory with no `keyring.json`; a header tamper in each of seven regions; `rewrapHeader`; and **no plaintext temp files**, with a `vi.mock('node:fs')` write-path spy asserting that the only file Node opens is `<name>.vbak.part` — plus a byte-level canary scan over every file the data directory holds afterwards. `backup.roundtrip` compares the two databases **logically** (`exportDomainState`), because `VACUUM INTO` renumbers free pages and two snapshots of the same data are different files and identical databases. **Four findings.** `08` §3.7.11's chunk-0 AD (`SHA-256(header bytes)`) contradicts its own `backup rewrap` three lines later — a rewrap replaces the slots, so an AD over the whole header makes every rewrapped archive undecryptable; it is now the core `{format, instanceId, createdAt, dataGen, body}`, and nothing is lost because every slot and keyring wrap is independently authenticated. The same section excluded the passphrase slot from the keyring snapshot, which made that slot open the body and not the database. `02` §3.14.4's step 8 now runs before step 7, so a failure aborts with nothing moved. And a path-traversal refusal raised with `next(error)` escaped as an **uncaught exception** rather than a handleable one. Also found and fixed on the way: **`M1-T14`'s pre-migration backup would have failed on every encrypted instance** — a keyless `quick_check` on a `VACUUM INTO` snapshot throws `SQLITE_NOTADB`, and the only test of that path seeded a plaintext database. The two file formats now share one framing (`stream-format.ts`), because `M1-T25` shipped a format bug caused by a second copy of it. 4085 tests total, 96.62% statements / 90.13% branches / 98.07% functions, 461 modules and 2039 dependencies with 0 depcruise violations, `pnpm verify` **exit 0** on Node **v26.13.0** — `a80621d`

#### Execution note

**Written 2026-11-03**, straight after the M0 gate (`CLAUDE.md` §1.4.5: no stop).

**What M1 is.** 35 tasks, and the one milestone where getting it wrong is expensive to undo: after
M1 there are migrations, key files and backup envelopes in real data directories. `14` §2 names the
never-cut set for exactly that reason — the spikes, keys, `VBK1` recovery-key restore, the outbox
and the queue.

**Spikes first, as the plan requires.** All three are blocking and all three are about native
modules, which is the risk M0 could not retire:

| Spike | Question | What I do if it fails |
|---|---|---|
| **SP-1** | Do `better-sqlite3-multiple-ciphers` (SQLCipher v4 raw key), `sqlite-vec`, FTS5 and Drizzle all work *together*, on Node 26 and 24, on darwin-arm64 / linux-x64 / linux-arm64 and in the Docker base? | Plain `better-sqlite3` + column-envelope encryption + HMAC-token FTS + an in-memory vector index (`02`). That is a materially worse product, so this gets the most attention |
| **SP-3** | Is `VACUUM INTO` output encrypted under SQLite3MC in WAL mode? | Online backup API, with archive-level encryption only |
| **SP-2** | Does `libsodium-wrappers` secretstream reach 40 MB/s on a Pi 5? | `sodium-native` (a build dependency, filed per A8) |

SP-1 and SP-3 are `M1-T2`; SP-2 is `M1-T4`. The arm64-Linux leg of SP-1 and the whole of SP-2's
threshold need hardware I do not have: that is owner ask **H-10**, and the substitution is written
down there rather than implied. **Nothing waits for it** — every other leg is measurable here and
in CI, and where a threshold decides a default I take the conservative branch and say so.

**Task order.** `14` §2's table order, with these deliberate shapes:

1. `M1-T1` (scaffold and pins) alone and first, because every later task imports from it and
   because `01` §14 A8/A11 boundary rules have to exist before there is anything to violate.
2. `M1-T2` (the spike) immediately after, before any schema is written. If SP-1 fails, the schema
   changes shape — writing it first would be writing it twice.
3. Then the crypto spine **serially**: `M1-T4` → `M1-T5` → `M1-T6`. Keys are the one subsystem where
   a subtle bug is unrecoverable for a host (a wrapped RDK you cannot unwrap is their data gone), and
   `CLAUDE.md` §1.5 forbids parallelising anything in this class.
4. `M1-T7` → `M1-T8` → `M1-T9` (data dir, open/init, `db.tx`). `M1-T9` is the transaction boundary
   the whole 1.0 depends on, and it brings the `no-await-in-tx` lint — a guard, so it gets a planted
   fixture like every other.
5. Schema `M1-T10` → `M1-T13` and the migration runner `M1-T14`, serially. `CLAUDE.md` §1.5:
   migrations are never parallelised.
6. `M1-T15` (test kit) before the repositories, so every repository is written against
   `createTestDb` from its first line rather than retrofitted.
7. `M1-T16` → `M1-T22` repositories, with `M1-T19`, `M1-T20` and `M1-T21` in parallel worktrees
   (disjoint files, no shared schema edits).
8. `M1-T23` (queue) and `M1-T24` (scheduler) serially on the main thread. The queue's model-based
   property suite at 20k runs is the single best test in this milestone and I want to be the one
   reading its counterexamples.
9. `M1-T25` → `M1-T28` (blobs, retention, backup, export) serially; `M1-T27`'s recovery-key-only
   restore is a gate item and a product promise.
10. `M1-T35` (docs) last, as the plan has it.

**Sub-agents (`‖`, separate worktrees, no commits from the agent).** `M1-T3` (IDs/clock/canonical
JSON), `M1-T7` (data-dir primitives), `M1-T29` (`TokenService`), `M1-T30` (`safeFetch`), `M1-T31`
(secret loading), `M1-T32` (bench infrastructure), `M1-T34` (Stryker), and `M1-T19`/`M1-T20`/`M1-T21`
as above. Ten of 35. Everything touching `packages/core`, the schema, migrations or the keyring
stays on the main thread.

`M1-T30` (`safeFetch`) is the one I would most like to hand off and will instead watch closely: it
is the egress guard the whole product's I5 invariant rests on, its spec asks for a ≥ 60-case SSRF
matrix, and M0's lesson is that this is precisely the kind of guard that passes its own tests while
letting traffic out.

**Risks I am taking in, and what I will do about them.**

- *Native modules on four platforms × two Node versions is the biggest single risk in the project.*
  It is why SP-1 is first. I will record every combination that works and every one that does not,
  including the ones that fail, because a matrix with holes in it is still evidence and a matrix
  with only successes in it is marketing.
- *`minimumReleaseAge: 3 days` plus exact pins means a named version may be unresolvable.* `02`
  M1-T1 names seven exact versions checked on 2026-11-01. `CLAUDE.md` §6 requires re-verification
  at implementation time; I will check each against the registry before pinning and log any that
  moved.
- *The no-await-in-tx rule is a custom lint rule, and M0 found five guards that were configured and
  inert.* It gets a planted fixture that fails `pnpm lint`, in the real tree, through the real
  command — and it goes into `pnpm test:guards`.
- *Coverage thresholds for `storage` are 85/75, lower than `shared`'s 90/80.* I will not treat that
  as the target. The parts where a bug is silent — the label join, the outbox state machine, the
  queue's lease arithmetic, the audit hash chain — get property tests, not percentage points.
- *`exportDomainState` and `computeEligible` both need `canRead`, which does not exist until M5.*
  The plan's answer is a **named** stub (`StubInformationFlow`, `StubRetraction`) that fails closed,
  re-run at `M5-T2`. Named and fail-closed, so it can never be mistaken for the real thing — and
  `14` §0.2 is explicit that this is the only acceptable kind of stub.

**Needed from the human.** **H-10** (a Pi 5 afternoon) is new and blocks nothing. **H-6** still has
the clock on it. Nothing else in M1 needs a credential: there is no model call, no platform API and
no network in this milestone — which is why it is the right place to be strict about `safeFetch`
before anything uses it.
